# agent_3: the whole run

[user message]
All graphs are finite, simple, and undirected. A graph is $H$-free if it contains no subgraph, not necessarily induced, isomorphic to $H$. Write $e(G)$ for the number of edges of $G$, and $c(G)$ for its number of simple cycles of length at least three, counted without orientation or a chosen starting vertex. For a fixed graph $H$, define
\[
\operatorname{ex}(n,H)=\max\{e(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\},
\qquad
\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)=\max\{c(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\}.
\]
Let $\chi(H)$ denote the minimum number of colors in a proper vertex coloring of $H$. Let $T_{n,r}$ be the complete $r$-partite graph on $n$ vertices whose part sizes differ by at most one, allowing empty parts, and put $t_r(n)=e(T_{n,r})$.

For each integer $r\ge2$, let $\mathcal S_r$ be the set of real numbers $a\ge0$ for which there exist a fixed graph $H$ with $\chi(H)=r+1$, a constant $C\ge0$, and a positive integer $N_0$ satisfying both conditions:

1. For every integer $N\ge N_0$,
\[
\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C.
\]
2. For infinitely many positive integers $n$, there is no $n$-vertex $H$-free graph $G$ satisfying simultaneously
\[
e(G)=\operatorname{ex}(n,H)
\quad\text{and}\quad
c(G)=\operatorname{cyc}(n,H).
\]

The choices of $H,C,N_0$ may depend on $r$ and $a$, but not on $N$ or $n$. Determine $\mathcal S_r$ explicitly for every integer $r\ge2$, including whether its finite boundary points belong to it.

The answer is \mathcal S_2=[0,\infty),\qquad \mathcal S_r=[1/r,\infty)\ \text{for }r\ge3.. This was proved recently, so it is not an open problem: your team's task is to prove it, completely, and to write Python that really computes or checks every step that can be checked. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at any other answer is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving it takes three claims: for every \(a\ge0\), there exist \(H,C,N_0\) with \(\chi(H)=3\) satisfying conditions 1 and 2; for every \(r\ge3\) and \(a\ge1/r\), there exist \(H,C,N_0\) with \(\chi(H)=r+1\) satisfying conditions 1 and 2; and for every \(r\ge3\) and \(0\le a<1/r\), every fixed graph \(H\) with \(\chi(H)=r+1\) for which \(\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C\) for some \(C\ge0\) and all sufficiently large \(N\) has, for every sufficiently large \(n\), an \(n\)-vertex \(H\)-free graph \(G\) satisfying both \(e(G)=\operatorname{ex}(n,H)\) and \(c(G)=\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)\). Split the work, and the pieces of each part, between you on Slack.

Stop only when the team's proof is complete. Before you stop, post on Slack what you proved, and what is left.

```
import networkx as nx,itertools,collections

def costs(G,r=3):
 n=len(G); adj=[sum(1<<v for v in G[u]) for u in range(n)]
 masks=[0]*r; best=99; witness=None
 def rec(v,used):
  nonlocal best,witness
  if v==n:
   cost=sum(any(adj[u]&m for u in range(n) if m>>u&1) for m in masks)
   if cost<best:best=cost;witness=masks.copy()
   return
  for c in range(min(used+1,r)):
   m=masks[c];nei=adj[v]&m
   if nei.bit_count()>1:continue
   if nei:
    u=nei.bit_length()-1
    if adj[u]&m:continue
   masks[c]|=1<<v;rec(v+1,max(used,c+1));masks[c]=m
 rec(0,0);return best,witness
found=[]
for G in nx.graph_atlas_g():
 if len(G)<4 or not nx.is_connected(G):continue
 b,w=costs(G)
 if b==3:
  print('FOUND',len(G),G.number_of_edges(),list(G.edges()),w); found.append(G)
print('count',len(found))

```

I'm thinking about how H can make cross edges to complete a bipartite graph, but only within corresponding colors. for r in range(3,11):

  poss=[]

"""Exact cycle counts of joins. All arithmetic is integral or rational.
The component polynomial Q counts unoriented path forests with orientations
on every nonsingleton path. Suitable for tiny internal graphs or disjoint
unions of repeatedly used fixed components.
"""
from math import factorial, comb
from fractions import Fraction
from itertools import combinations

def mul(a,b):
 c=[0]*(len(a)+len(b)-1)
 for i,x in enumerate(a):
  for j,y in enumerate(b): c[i+j]+=x*y
 return c

def power(a,n):
 out=[1]
 while n:
  if n&1: out=mul(out,a)
  a=mul(a,a);n//=2
 return out

def path_polynomial(n,edges):
 # Exhaustive verified directly for graphs with few internal edges.
 ans=[comb(n,k) for k in range(n+1)]
 for mask in range(1,1<<len(edges)):
  deg=[0]*n; parent=list(range(n)); selected=set();ok=True
  def find(x):
   while parent[x]!=x:
    x=parent[x]
   return x
  for i,(u,v) in enumerate(edges):
   if not (mask>>i)&1:continue
   deg[u]+=1;deg[v]+=1
   if deg[u]>2 or deg[v]>2 or find(u)==find(v):ok=False;break
   parent[find(u)]=find(v);selected.update((u,v))
  if not ok:continue
  components=len(selected)-mask.bit_count()
  for isolated in range(n-len(selected)+1):
   ans[components+isolated]+=(1<<components)*comb(n-len(selected),isolated)
 return ans

def _weights(q):return [factorial(k)*v for k,v in enumerate(q)]

def cycles_join_r2(q1,q2,n1,n2,internal_cycles=0):
 w1,w2=_weights(q1),_weights(q2)
 s=sum(Fraction(w1[k]*w2[k],k) for k in range(1,min(len(w1),len(w2))))
 ans=(s-n1*n2)/2+internal_cycles
 assert ans.denominator==1
 return int(ans)

def cycles_join_r3(q1,q2,q3,sizes,internal_cycles=0):
 # -log(1-xy-xz-yz-2xyz), multinomial expansion.
 w1,w2,w3=map(_weights,(q1,q2,q3));n1,n2,n3=sizes
 fac=[factorial(i) for i in range(n1+n2+n3+1)]
 total=Fraction(0)
 for d in range(min(n1,n2,n3)+1):
  for a in range(min(n1-d,n2-d)+1):
   for b in range(min(n1-a-d,n3-d)+1):
    for c in range(min(n2-a-d,n3-b-d)+1):
     k=a+b+c+d
     if not k: continue
     i,j,l=a+b+d,a+c+d,b+c+d
     num=fac[k-1]*(1<<d)*w1[i]*w2[j]*w3[l]
     den=fac[a]*fac[b]*fac[c]*fac[d]
     total+=Fraction(num,den)
 ans=(total-n1*n2-n1*n3-n2*n3)/2+internal_cycles
 assert ans.denominator==1
 return int(ans)

def brute_cycles(n,edges):
 adj=[set() for _ in range(n)]
 for u,v in edges:adj[u].add(v);adj[v].add(u)
 count=0
 for start in range(n):
  def walk(path,used):
   nonlocal count
   for v in adj[path[-1]]:
    if v==start:
     if len(path)>=3:count+=1
    elif v>start and not ((used>>v)&1):walk(path+[v],used|(1<<v))
  walk([start],1<<start)
 return count//2

def check():
 assert path_polynomial(2,[(0,1)])==[1,4,1]
 assert path_polynomial(3,[(0,1),(1,2),(0,2)])==[1,15,9,1]
 # Exhaustively test every graph on <=4 vertices as first joined class,
 # and <=2 isolated vertices in each of 1 or 2 other classes.
 checked=0
 for n in range(1,5):
  poss=list(combinations(range(n),2))
  for mask in range(1<<len(poss)):
   es=[e for i,e in enumerate(poss) if mask>>i&1]
   q=path_polynomial(n,es);internal=brute_cycles(n,es)
   for m in range(1,3):
    edges=es+[(i,n+j) for i in range(n) for j in range(m)]
    got=cycles_join_r2(q,power([1,1],m),n,m,internal)
    assert got==brute_cycles(n+m,edges),(n,m,es,got)
    checked+=1
    for l in range(1,3):
     edges3=edges+[(i,n+m+j) for i in range(n+m) for j in range(l)]
     got=cycles_join_r3(q,power([1,1],m),power([1,1],l),(n,m,l),internal)
     assert got==brute_cycles(n+m+l,edges3),(n,m,l,es,got)
     checked+=1
 return checked
if __name__=='__main__':print('exact cycle join checks:',check())

def qp(m,e):

from pathlib import Path
code='''"""Checked boundary construction ingredients; does not prove exact Turan structure."""
import math,itertools,networkx as nx
from agent1_cycles import path_polynomial,power,cycles_join_r3

def sun():
 g=nx.Graph();g.add_nodes_from(range(6));g.add_edges_from([(0,1),(1,2),(2,0),(0,3),(1,3),(1,4),(2,4),(2,5),(0,5)])
 return g

def lifted_sun(r,M):
 h=sun();start=6
 for j in range(r-2):
  old=list(h);new=list(range(start,start+M));start+=M
  h.add_nodes_from(new);h.add_edges_from((u,v) for u in old for v in new)
 return h

def star_poly(L):
 # empty center case: arbitrary subset of L-1 leaves; selected center
 # may have 0,1,2 selected incident edges in a path forest.
 from agent1_cycles import mul
 q=power([1,1],L)
 for deg in (1,2):
  orient=2
  base=[0,orient*math.comb(L-1,deg)]
  more=power([1,1],L-1-deg)
  terms=mul(base,more)
  for i,x in enumerate(terms):q[i]+=x
 return q

def run_checks():
 s=sun()
 assert all(not nx.is_bipartite(nx.subgraph(s,[v for v in s if v!=u])) for u in s)
 # Verify every 2-host assignment of sun forces either internal triangle/P4
 # (one host independent).
 checked=0
 for mask in range(1<<6):
  A=[v for v in s if mask>>v&1];B=[v for v in s if not mask>>v&1]
  if s.subgraph(B).number_of_edges():continue
  f=s.subgraph(A)
  triangle=any(len(c)==3 for c in nx.enumerate_all_cliques(f))
  p4=any(all(f.has_edge(p[i],p[i+1]) for i in range(3)) for p in itertools.permutations(A,4))
  assert triangle or p4
  checked+=1
 for L in range(2,12):
  assert star_poly(L)==path_polynomial(L,[(0,j) for j in range(1,L)])
 # Exact rational-free construction inequality from log integral bound.
 for r in range(3,1001):
  L=2*r;s=r-1
  assert 2*(1-1/L)/s>2*math.log1p(1/s)
 print('sun partitions checked:',checked,'; star polynomial identities:10; log inequalities:998')
 for k in range(1,5):
  m=6*k;n=3*m
  q=power(star_poly(6),k);qbig=star_poly(m);iso=power([1,1],m)
  small=cycles_join_r3(q,iso,iso,(m,m,m))
  big=cycles_join_r3(qbig,iso,iso,(m,m,m))
  print('n=',n,'bounded-star cycles=',small,'universal cycles=',big,'ratio=',float(small/big))
  if k>=2:assert small>big
if __name__=='__main__':run_checks()
'''

from functools import lru_cache
@lru_cache(None)
def iso(n):return power([1,1],n)
@lru_cache(None)
def mat(n):return mul(power([1,4,1],n//2),power([1,1],n%2))
for n in range(12,81):
 poss=[]
 for a in range(max(1,n//3-3),n//3+5):
  for b in range(a,n//3+5):
   c=n-a-b
   if c<1:continue
   ed=a*b+a*c+b*c+a//2+b//2
   poss.append((ed,a,b,c))
 em=max(p[0] for p in poss);best=(-1,None);bestedge=(-1,None)

print('done')

def forest_kind(adj,A):
 seen=0;forest=True;sf=True
 for v in range(8):
  if not(A>>v&1) or seen>>v&1:continue
  todo=[v];seen|=1<<v;deg=[]
  while todo:
   u=todo.pop();nb=adj[u]&A;deg.append(nb.bit_count())
   while nb:
    bit=nb&-nb;nb-=bit
    if not seen&bit:seen|=bit;todo.append(bit.bit_length()-1)
  if sum(deg)!=2*(len(deg)-1):return False,False
  if sum(d>1 for d in deg)>1:sf=False
 return True,sf
for remn in range(1,9):

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# Boundary construction for r>=3

Use the following standard form of Simonovits' decomposition theorem: if chi(H)=r+1 and H embeds in the join of a fixed path and r-1 sufficiently large independent sets, some edge extremizer lies in D(n,r,s), for a fixed s. In D, deleting at most s exceptional vertices leaves r near-balanced classes, each consisting of repeated mutually nonadjacent copies of one connected graph of order at most s, symmetric outside the copies. If the edge extremizer within D is unique up to isomorphism, it is globally unique. The exact theorem statement/reference must be supplied in the final proof.

Let F have vertices a,b,c,d,e,f, edges ad,ae,af,bc,be,bf,cd,ce,de. Equivalently it is a 5-wheel with the spoke ef removed. For L=2r and M=L+1 put H=F joined to r-2 independent M-sets, joined pairwise completely. Then chi(H)=r+1. Every F-v remains nonbipartite: deleting e leaves C5, deleting any cycle vertex leaves a triangle. Thus H has no color-critical vertex.

The six maximal independent sets of F are ab,ac,bd,cf,df,ef. Their complements contain respectively triangle cde, path d-e-b-f, path f-a-e-c, triangle ade, triangle bce, path a-d-c-b. Hence F embeds in the join of a graph J with a sufficiently large independent set iff J contains a triangle or P4. In particular, star forests are forbidden-F-free in this host. The reverse direction here uses the explicit decompositions: outside ab leaves triangle cde plus isolated f; outside bd leaves P4 f-a-e-c.

Also F embeds complete bipartite plus matchings on both sides: parts abc and def have respectively only edges bc and de. Finally F-{e,f} is bipartite and e,f are nonadjacent.

## D uniqueness
For an extremizer in D, each normal connected block must be a star: any triangle or P4 embeds H using independent reservoirs in the other normal classes. At most one normal class has nontrivial blocks, by the two-matching embedding. Suppose that class has blocks of order k>=2. Its normal internal edge contribution is at most (1-1/k)n/r+O(1). If an exceptional vertex sees a positive fraction of the active class and sees all other normal classes, it creates P4 with two star blocks, embedding H; thus every exceptional vertex has weighted normal degree at most (r-1)n/r+O(1). Consequently e(G)<=t_r(n)+(1-1/k)n/r+O(1), contradicting the H-free graph U_n=K1 join T_r(n-1), which has t_r(n)+n/r+O(1) edges.

Thus all normal blocks are singletons. Every exceptional vertex sees all or none of each normal class. At most one sees every class: two such vertices embed H by putting e,f there and using the bipartition of F-{e,f} plus the r-2 join classes. All other exception vertices see at most r-1 normal classes. To attain the lower bound from U_n, exactly one universal-type exception u exists and every other exception misses exactly one class. Assign each ordinary exception to its missing class.

Within the resulting r classes, all edges involve boundedly many ordinary exceptions, and each internal graph is a star forest (otherwise P4/triangle). If an internal edge xy has either endpoint adjacent to u, it gives a P4 together with u and a fresh normal vertex of its class (or a triangle), again embedding H. Hence u is nonadjacent to every vertex incident to an internal edge. Let B be its set of ordinary nonneighbors. Then total internal edges <=|B|-1 whenever any exists; in all cases internal edges-|B|<=0, equality only if both are empty. All missing cross-edges cause further loss. Therefore
 e(G)<=t_r(n-1)+(n-1),
with equality only for U_n. This proves unique D extremizer, hence globally unique extremizer by the quoted theorem. In particular ex(n,H)=t_r(n)+ceil(n/r)-1<=t_r(n)+n/r.

## Competing cycles
For n divisible by rL, take balanced r classes and put disjoint L-vertex stars in the first class, leaving all other classes independent; call this V_n. It is H-free. Indeed if an M-vertex join class of H has no vertex in a pure host class, it spans at least two distinct star components. Every other H-vertex, adjacent to all its M vertices, must then avoid the modified class; but H minus that join class has chromatic number r and cannot fit r-1 pure classes. Thus all r-2 join classes use distinct pure classes, leaving F at most one pure class, impossible by the six independent-set checks.

The standard multipartite local-limit/path-forest expansion gives, for r>=3, with p=(r-1)/r,
 c(U_n)/c(T_r(n)) -> p^(-2),
 c(V_n)/c(T_r(n)) -> exp(2(1-1/L)/(r-1)).
For clarity, contraction of k fixed disjoint internal edges multiplies Hamilton-cycle counts asymptotically by [2r/((r-1)n)]^k; bounded-degree forests yield a Poisson sum with mean 2r e(F_n)/((r-1)n). Adjacent internal edge selections are lower-order. Open color-word counts give p^(-2) for U_n. Passing from Hamilton cycles to all cycles multiplies both by exp(1/p), since deleting j vertices contributes asymptotically p^(-j)/j!.

For s=r-1,
 1-s log(1+1/s) = s int_0^(1/s) t/(1+t) dt > 1/[2(s+1)] = 1/L.
So the second limiting ratio is strictly larger. Therefore V_n eventually has more cycles than the unique edge extremizer U_n, for infinitely many n. This establishes a=1/r and all larger a.

The executed checker workspace/agent3_boundary.py verifies every finite F claim and the star path polynomial, and computes exact positive cycle gaps at r=3,n=108,144,180 using the separately brute-force-verified join formula.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_boundary_proof.md').write_text(text)
print('saved',len(text))

```

import sys,networkx as nx,itertools,json
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent1_cycles import brute_cycles
results=[];checked=0
for g in nx.graph_atlas_g():
 n=len(g)
 if n<4 or min(dict(g.degree()).values())<=n/2:continue
 c=brute_cycles(n,list(g.edges()))
 for u,v in nx.non_edges(g):
  new=brute_cycles(n,list(g.edges())+[(u,v)])
  val=n*(new-c)/c
  assert val>0
  results.append((val,n,nx.to_graph6_bytes(g,header=False).decode().strip(),[u,v]));checked+=1
print('checked robust Dirac edge additions',checked)
print('min',min(results),'max',max(results))
from pathlib import Path
Path('workspace/agent3_dirac_checks.json').write_text(json.dumps({'count':checked,'min':min(results),'max':max(results)}))

```

from pathlib import Path
s=r'''# Coarse cycle normalization via Dirac supergraphs

## Dirac edge-addition lemma
For every gamma>0 there exist positive constants c_gamma,C_gamma and n0 such that if delta(F)>=(1/2+gamma)n, n>=n0, and xy is absent, then
 c_gamma/n <= (c(F+xy)-c(F))/c(F) <= C_gamma/n.

Proof input: the classical weak Dirac Hamilton-count bound h(F)>=(a_gamma n)^n. This follows, for example, from Cuckler--Kahn; only this weak consequence is needed.

1. Hamilton path endpoint counts in a graph of minimum degree >=(1/2+gamma)m differ by at most a constant factor. Change one endpoint x to u by the two-cut switching described in agent1 Slack #136. The two arcs cut by the edge following u together supply >=2delta-m-O(1)>=gamma m admissible pivots. Every output has O(m) preimages. Reverse the comparison and change the second endpoint as needed.
2. Write P_ab for unoriented Hamilton paths with endpoints a,b. Since sum_{ab in E}P_ab=m h(F), endpoint comparability implies P_xy=Theta_gamma(h(F)/m) for ALL distinct x,y, adjacent or not.
3. Cycles/paths of length <=(1-gamma/2)n number at most n!/(floor(gamma n/2))! times a polynomial, and hence are negligible compared with h(F)/n^A for every fixed A.
4. Any long cycle omitting a fixed vertex v has Omega_gamma(n) insertion positions for v: number of its edges with both ends adjacent to v is at least 2d_C(v)-|C|. Each resulting cycle has one inverse deletion. Thus cycles omitting x or y are O_gamma(c(F)/n).
5. For every long vertex set S containing x,y, F[S] remains uniformly Dirac. Apply step 2 to F[S], then sum over S. This gives the displayed lemma.

## Eliminating all coarse bad vertices
Suppose a cycle maximizer G has an r-partition, r>=3, with class sizes n/r+o(n), total missing cross-edges M=o(n^2), and maximum internal degree eta*n=o(n). Such a partition should come from ordinary stability plus the color-critical-vertex embedding argument.

Choose tau->0 with eta=o(tau), M/n^2=o(tau^2). Let B be vertices with at least tau*n missing cross-neighbors, b=|B|. Then b<=2M/(tau*n)=o(tau*n). Let G* replace all B vertices by ordinary vertices in their assigned classes: remove every internal edge incident with B; fill every cross-edge incident with B. Good vertices have missing cross-degree <tau*n. If H has a color-critical vertex and the partition is chosen as usual, G* is H-free by greedily replacing every newly added ordinary H-vertex by an unused good vertex of the same class; required same-class edges never involve the new vertices, and each cross-neighbor constraint excludes only o(n) choices.

Let J=G union G*. Both J and G* have minimum degree (1-1/r)n-o(n), so they are uniformly Dirac. The number D of edges in J-G* satisfies
 D<=eta*n*b+b^2=o(tau*n*b).
By the upper Dirac edge-addition bound,
 log(c(J)/c(G*))<=C D/n=o(tau*b).

Each bad vertex has at least (1-o(1))*tau*n missing GOOD cross-neighbors in G. Choose exactly floor(tau*n/2) of these for each bad vertex, obtaining an edge set A subset E(J)-E(G), with |A|=(1+o(1))*tau*n*b/2. Put K=J-A. Then G is a subgraph of K. Moreover K is uniformly Dirac: bad vertices lose only tau*n/2 from degree (1-1/r)n-o(n), and good vertices lose at most b=o(n). Every intermediate graph from K to J remains uniformly Dirac. The lower Dirac edge-addition bound yields
 log(c(J)/c(K))>=c |A|/n >=c' tau*b.
Since c(G)<=c(K), combining gives
 log(c(G*)/c(G))>=c'tau*b-o(tau*b)>0
whenever b>0, a contradiction. Thus the cycle maximizer has maximum missing cross-degree o(n).

No path kernels involving bad endpoints, no bad-cluster expansion, and no asymptotic 1+o(1) endpoint estimates are needed.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_normalization_lemma.md').write_text(s)
print('saved',len(s))

```

G=nx.from_graph6_bytes(b'K`?Ij_SJIo?_')

opts=[]
for mask in range(1<<len(G)):
 I=[i for i in G if mask>>i&1]
 if G.subgraph(I).number_of_edges():continue
 F=G.subgraph([i for i in G if not(mask>>i&1)])
 if not nx.is_forest(F):continue
 comps=[F.subgraph(c) for c in nx.connected_components(F)]
 if any(nx.diameter(c)>3 for c in comps):continue
 req=[]
 for c in comps:
  high=sorted([d for u,d in c.degree() if d>=2])
  if len(high)>2:print('err')
  if len(high)==2:req.append(tuple(high))
  elif len(high)==1:req.append((1,high[0]))
  elif c.number_of_edges():req.append((1,1))
  else:req.append((0,0))
 opts.append((I,req))

for D in range(3,24):

  print('D',D,'k',k,'tops',tops)

from pathlib import Path
p=Path('workspace/agent3_normalization_lemma.md')
s=p.read_text()
s+=r'''

## Obtaining the initial stability partition without sharp edge estimates
Let p=(r-1)/r. Since T_r(n) is H-free, a cycle maximizer G satisfies
 log c(G) >= n log(p*n)-n-o(n).
The multipartite Hamilton-cycle local limit estimate suffices for this bound.

The permanent of I+A(G) is at least 1+2c(G): every individual cycle, in either orientation, together with fixed points elsewhere is a permutation counted by the permanent. By Bregman's theorem,
 log per(I+A) <= sum_v log((d(v)+1)!)/(d(v)+1).
For every fixed L, the number k_L of vertices with d(v)<=L is O_L(n/log n). Indeed those rows contribute at most a fixed constant per row, while every other row contributes at most log(n)-1+epsilon_L, with epsilon_L bounded; comparison with the lower bound forces k_L*log n=O_L(n).

Uniform Stirling gives
 log(s!)/s = log s-1+O(log(s+1)/s).
The total error here is o(n): first remove the o(n) bounded-degree rows, then let L tend to infinity. Jensen therefore gives
 log per(I+A) <= n log((2e(G)+n)/n)-n+o(n).
Comparing with c(T_r(n)) yields e(G)>=p*n^2/2-o(n^2).

Ordinary Erdos--Simonovits stability now yields r near-balanced classes with o(n^2) internal edges and missing cross-edges. Remove o(n) bad vertices so that every remaining good vertex has both internal degree o(n) and missing cross-degree o(n), uniformly.

Let h be a color-critical vertex of H and fix an r-coloring of H-h. For every epsilon>0, no vertex v can have at least epsilon*n GOOD neighbors in every one of the r classes, for then greedily embedding each color class of H-h into those good neighbors (avoiding at most |H|*o(n) cross-nonneighbors at each choice) embeds H with h mapped to v. Uniformly, every vertex therefore has o(n) neighbors in some good class. Reassign each bad vertex to such a class. Only o(n) vertices move, so the classes remain balanced and cross-deficit remains o(n^2); every vertex now has internal degree o(n).

This supplies exactly the hypotheses of the supergraph-normalization argument above. No O(n log n) edge deficit or O(log n) exceptional-set bound is required.
'''
p.write_text(s)
print('updated normalization proof',len(s))

```

from pathlib import Path
p=Path('workspace/agent3_normalization_lemma.md');s=p.read_text()
s+=r'''

## Exact cross-completion once internal components are bounded
Assume now that all internal components have at most K vertices and only finitely many isomorphism types, as supplied by agent2's factor-packing lemma; absorb its O(1) exceptional components into the bound K.

A cycle maximizer is H-saturated, because it is uniformly Dirac and every added edge lies on a new Hamilton cycle. For any internal component type occurring linearly many times, every such component is cross-complete to EVERY vertex. Otherwise add a missing cross-edge xy with x in one such component C. In the resulting H-copy, replace all selected vertices of C by the corresponding vertices in a fresh component of the same type. At most |H|*o(n) components are excluded by the cross-nonneighbors of the other H-vertices, so a fresh suitable component exists; this reproduces H in G, contradiction. Every class has at least one linear-multiplicity type, hence an unbounded supply of vertices cross-complete to all other classes, chosen from distinct components when needed.

For any internal component C, define G_C by deleting its internal edges and filling all its missing cross-edges. This remains H-free: if a new H-copy used a vertex of C, that vertex has no required same-class neighbors, and can be replaced by a fresh reservoir vertex from another component of the same class. Reservoir vertices are cross-complete even to exceptional vertices, so all required edges survive. The union J=G union G_C and G_C are uniformly Dirac. The edge-addition lemma shows
 log(c(J)/c(G)) >= c_gamma*m(C)/n+O(m(C)/n^2),
 log(c(J)/c(G_C)) <= C_gamma*K^2/n+O(1/n^2).
Thus cycle maximality forces m(C)<=D for a fixed constant D (choose D>2C_gamma*K^2/c_gamma). In particular every vertex has at most D missing cross-neighbors.

Repeat the saturation-and-fresh-copy argument. Now a type with more than |H|*D+|H| components is cross-complete to every vertex: only |H|*D vertices, hence at most that many components, can be prohibited by fixed H-vertices. All types with fewer copies together occupy O(1) vertices. Put these in R. Outside R, the graph is an exact complete join of r classes, each a disjoint union of bounded trees; cross-nonedges lie only in R by R. There can be several common tree types in a class.

Finally the upper bound with a<1/r forces H to embed in the complete r-partite graph augmented by matchings in TWO classes. Therefore two distinct classes cannot both contain arbitrarily many nontrivial common components: choose the finitely many required matching edges and extra vertices in distinct components, then fill the other independent classes from reservoirs. Consequently, after increasing R by O(1), at most one normal class contains nontrivial trees.

This proves the required finite-template reduction, but DOES NOT prove edge-priority between different rare templates R. The team has exact examples showing that bounded internal forests alone do not imply that priority. The remaining argument must use the fact that all cells arise from one forbidden H with a color-critical vertex and ex<t_r+n/r.
'''
p.write_text(s);print('length',len(s))

```

# A rough Hamilton-cycle count in dense Dirac graphs

**Lemma.** For every fixed gamma>0 there is C_gamma such that every sufficiently
large n-vertex graph F with minimum degree at least (1/2+gamma)n satisfies
h(F) >= n! exp(-C_gamma n), where h counts unoriented Hamilton cycles.

Inputs are only hypergeometric concentration, the integral max-flow theorem,
van der Waerden's permanent theorem, and the directed Dirac theorem
(minimum in- and outdegree at least m/2 implies a directed Hamilton cycle).

First suppose N is divisible by m=ceil(A_gamma log n), with N=n-O(log n).
A uniformly random ordered partition of V(F) into ell=N/m equal blocks is,
with probability at least 1/2, such that every vertex has at least
(1/2+gamma/4)m neighbors in every block. Indeed each count is hypergeometric;
the mean is at least (1/2+gamma/2)m and a union bound over at most n^2
vertex/block pairs applies for sufficiently large A_gamma. Removing the
O(log n) exceptional vertices initially only changes gamma to gamma/2.

A bipartite graph with m vertices on each side and minimum degree
Delta>(1/2+eta)m has an r-factor for r=floor(eta*m/2), for large m.
Here is an explicit max-flow cut verification. More generally suppose
r<=Delta/2 and r<=2Delta-m. For X,Y on the two sides, put x=|X|, y=|Y|,
s=x+y-m. Only s>0 needs checking. Assume x<=y.
* If x>=Delta, then y>=Delta, so e(X,Y)>=x(2Delta-m)>=rs.
* If r<=x<=Delta, then e(X,Y)>=x(Delta-m+y)=x(Delta-x+s)>=rs.
* If x<r, then y>m-r, so e(X,Y)>=x(Delta-r)>=xr>=rs.
These are exactly the r-factor cut inequalities. The r-factor has at least
r^m m!/m^m >= (r/e)^m perfect matchings by van der Waerden.
Thus each consecutive pair of blocks admits at least (c_gamma*m)^m matchings.

Choose such a matching freely between blocks i and i+1 for i=1,...,ell-1.
Their union consists of m vertex-disjoint paths, each with one vertex in every
block. Make a digraph on these m paths: put an arc i->j when the last vertex
of path i is adjacent to the first vertex of path j. After deleting possible
loops, its minimum in/outdegree is still greater than m/2. The directed Dirac
theorem supplies a directed Hamilton cycle, and hence a closing matching
which makes the union ONE Hamilton cycle of F.

There are at least N!/(2(m!)^ell) good ordered partitions. Each resulting
Hamilton cycle is compatible with at most 2N ordered partitions, since a
starting vertex and direction determine the repeating block labels. Its
first ell-1 matchings are then determined. Consequently
 h(F) >= N!/(4N(m!)^ell) * (c_gamma*m)^(N-m)
       >= N! * exp(-O_gamma(N)),
using m!<=m^m and m log m=o(N).

For arbitrary n, set aside a fixed set R of fewer than m vertices so that
N=n-|R| is divisible by m. Apply the preceding argument to F-R. Insert the
vertices of R one at a time, in a fixed order. At each stage every cycle has
at least 2d_C(v)-|C| >= gamma*n admissible insertion edges. Removing the last
inserted vertex is a unique inverse, so the number of final cycles is at
least h(F-R)*(gamma*n)^|R| >= n!*exp(-O_gamma(n)).

**Tail consequence.** The number of cycles, or paths with specified endpoints,
of length at most n-n/sqrt(log n) is at most
 n! * exp(-Omega(n sqrt(log n))).
This is negligible compared with the preceding Hamilton lower bound, even
after multiplication by any polynomial in n. Thus dense-Dirac all-cycle
switching arguments may discard such short cycles uniformly.

from pathlib import Path
s=r'''# Multipartite cycle asymptotics used by the boundary construction

Fix r>=3, p=(r-1)/r, and let n_i=n/r+O(1). Let A_cyc(n_1,...,n_r) count color strings at labeled cyclic positions 1,...,n, with no equal consecutive colors (including n and 1), using color i exactly n_i times. Let A_open denote the corresponding linear strings. Then for a constant K_r>0,
 A_cyc = K_r*(r-1)^n*n^(-(r-1)/2)*(1+o(1)),
 A_open/A_cyc -> r/(r-1).
The errors are uniform when the bounded displacements range over any fixed finite set.

Here is a Fourier proof, so no unquoted graph counting theorem is needed. Let M(z)_{ij}=z_j for i!=j and 0 otherwise. The two generating functions are tr M(z)^n and 1^T diag(z) M(z)^(n-1)1. Extract coefficients with z_i=exp(i theta_i), theta_r=0. At theta=0, M has simple eigenvalue r-1 and remaining eigenvalues -1. Its normalized Perron chain is the stationary uniform Markov chain which chooses uniformly a color different from its current color. Every mean-zero function has transition eigenvalue rho=-1/(r-1). Hence its occupation covariance on the sum-zero subspace is
 Sigma=(1/r)*(1+rho)/(1-rho)*I=(r-2)/r^2*I,
which is positive definite for r>=3. Analytic perturbation gives the usual quadratic expansion of the leading eigenvalue; the Gaussian integral supplies K_r. Bounded count displacements affect the integrand only by a phase tending to 1 in the n^(-1/2) saddle neighborhood. Away from theta=0, the spectral radius is strictly below r-1: equality in the triangle inequality would require phases z_j*w_j=omega*w_i for every i!=j; using three distinct colors forces all w_i equal and all z_i equal, so theta_r=0 forces every theta_i=0. Compactness makes the outside integral exponentially negligible. Finally the leading amplitudes at theta=0 are 1 for the trace and r/(r-1) for the open-string generating function. This proves both formulas by the Gaussian saddle calculation.

Since
 h(K_{n_1,...,n_r})=A_cyc*prod_i(n_i!)/(2n),
fixed contractions/deletions give the following ratios. For balanced n_i=m=n/r, and fixed k,
 h(K_{m-k,m,...,m})/h(T_r(n)) ~ [r/((r-1)n)]^k.
For fixed deletions j_i, j=sum j_i, the analogous ratio is [r/((r-1)n)]^j. The same formulas hold for O(1)-imbalanced starting classes.

## Bounded-degree insertions in one class
Let F_n be an internal graph in the first class, maximum degree bounded, with e(F_n)/n -> beta. A Hamilton cycle uses a path forest L of internal edges. Contracting its k edges yields the complete multipartite graph with first class m-k; each nontrivial path has two orientations. Thus exactly
 h(T_r(n)+F_n)=sum_{L path forest in F_n} 2^(number of nontrivial paths of L)*h(K_{m-e(L),m,...,m}).
For fixed k, all but O(n^(k-1)) selected k-edge forests are matchings; their number is e(F_n)^k/k!+O(n^(k-1)). Therefore the k-edge contribution divided by h(T_r(n)) tends to
 [2r*beta/(r-1)]^k/k!.

Domination for ALL k is elementary. Reinsert k labeled ordinary vertices of the first class into a Hamilton cycle of K_{m-k,m,...,m}. At each stage at least n-2m=(r-2)n/r cycle edges have both ends outside the first class, and insertion into each gives a valid distinct Hamilton cycle. Therefore
 h(K_{m-k,m,...,m})/h(T_r(n)) <= [(r-2)n/r]^(-k).
There are at most binom(e(F_n),k) path forests of k edges, and orientation multiplicity is at most 2^k. Hence the k-edge terms are dominated by C^k/k!, for a constant C. Dominated convergence gives
 h(T_r(n)+F_n)/h(T_r(n)) -> exp(2r*beta/(r-1)).
The proof is unchanged for O(1) class-size displacements or deleting O(1) vertices of F_n.

## One universal vertex
Let U_n=K1 join T_r(n-1), and take n divisible by r. Its Hamilton cycles correspond to unoriented Hamilton paths of T_r(n-1), so
 h(U_n)=A_open(m-1,m,...,m)*(m-1)!*(m!)^(r-1)/2.
Using the open/closed color-string ratios above gives
 h(U_n)/h(T_r(n)) -> (r/(r-1))^2=p^(-2).

## Passing from Hamilton cycles to all cycles
For both families above, fixed deletions of j ordinary vertices give
 h(G-S)/h(G) ~ (1/(p*n))^j.
Summing over choices of S gives fixed-j contribution p^(-j)/j!. In U_n the choices including its single universal vertex contribute only O(1/n) at every fixed j.

For domination, these graphs have minimum degree p*n-O(1). For j<=gamma*n, with gamma<(2p-1)/2 fixed, insert any fixed set S of j omitted vertices in a fixed order into a Hamilton cycle of G-S. At each step at least gamma*n edges have both ends adjacent to the inserted vertex, so
 binom(n,j)*max_{|S|=j} h(G-S)/h(G) <= gamma^(-j)/j!.
For larger j the total number of cycles is at most n!/(floor(gamma*n))! times a polynomial, negligible relative to h(G)>=n!*exp(-O(n)). Thus dominated convergence gives
 c(G)/h(G) -> exp(1/p).
The factor is the same for both boundary families. Consequently the two Hamilton-cycle ratios above also hold for total-cycle ratios.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_cycle_asymptotics.md').write_text(s);print('saved',len(s))

```

content:
```
from pathlib import Path
s=r'''# Rigorous first-order cycle comparison for the proposed counterphase

Let T=T_r(n), n=rm, r>=3 fixed. Put bounded-degree forests F_n,F'_n inside its first class. Assume
 e(F_n)/n,e(F'_n)/n -> beta>0,
 e(F_n)-e(F'_n)=d (fixed),
 (W(F_n)-W(F'_n))/n -> omega,
where W(F)=sum_v binom(d_F(v),2). Then
 n*(c(T+F'_n)/c(T+F_n)-1) -> -2*alpha*d+2*alpha^2*omega,
 alpha=r/(r-1).
This statement concerns ONLY bounded-degree forests in one class; it does not assert that fewer edges imply more cycles generally.

## Exact path-forest decomposition
For integers k>=1 and 1<=t<=k, let N_{k,t}(F) count k-edge path forests in F with t nontrivial components. Let R_{k,t}(n) count cycles of K_{m-k,m,...,m} containing t specified vertices of the first class. Contracting each chosen path gives exactly
 c(T+F)=c(T)+sum_{k,t} 2^t N_{k,t}(F) R_{k,t}(n).
For fixed k,t,
 R_{k,t}(n)/c(T) ~ (alpha/n)^k.
Indeed the ordinary complete-multipartite contraction ratio follows from the color-word Fourier calculation in agent3_cycle_asymptotics.md; requiring finitely many specified vertices changes the answer by 1+O(1/n), by the insertion bound for omitted fixed vertices.

For fixed k, bounded-degree overlap counting gives
 N_{k,k}(F)=binom(e,k)-W*binom(e-2,k-2)+O_k(n^(k-2)),
 N_{k,k-1}(F)=W*binom(e-2,k-2)+O_k(n^(k-2)),
and sum_{t<=k-2}N_{k,t}(F)=O_k(n^(k-2)).
A nonmatching choice contains an adjacent edge pair; two or more overlaps have O(n^(k-2)) choices. Thus the k-th contribution to n*(c(T+F')-c(T+F))/c(T) tends to
 2^k*alpha^k * [ -d*beta^(k-1)/(k-1)! + (omega/2)*beta^(k-2)/(k-2)! ],
with the second summand zero for k=1.

## Uniform summation justification
The nonmatching count is at most W*binom(e-2,k-2), by marking an adjacent pair. Also
 |binom(e,k)-binom(e-d,k)| <= |d|*binom(max(e,e-d),k-1).
Consequently the difference of the matching counts, and the sum of all nonmatching counts in both forests, are at most
 poly(k)*C^k*n^(k-1)/k!
for a constant C independent of n,k.

Choose fixed epsilon>0 sufficiently small. For k<=epsilon*n, the contracted complete multipartite graph is uniformly Dirac. In any graph of minimum degree at least (1/2+gamma)N, one has c(G)<=C_gamma*h(G): insert any omitted j vertices in fixed order, yielding sum_{j<=gamma*N} gamma^(-j)/j!, while the remaining short cycles are negligible by the rough Dirac Hamilton-count bound proved in agent2_dirac_count.md. Hence
 R_{k,t}(n)/c(T) <= C_gamma*h(K_{m-k,m,...,m})/h(T)
 <= C_gamma*[(r-2)n/r]^(-k),
where the last inequality is the exact ordinary-vertex reinsertion bound. After multiplying by n, the difference terms are therefore dominated by poly(k)*C_1^k/k!, a summable series.

For k>epsilon*n, bound each forest separately. There are at most binom(C*n,k) choices and at most 2^k orientations. The contracted graph has n-k vertices, so its number of cycles is at most C_2*(n-k)!. Meanwhile c(T)>=n!*p^n*n^(-C_3), where p=(r-1)/r. Since k<=m and (n-k)!/n!<=(p*n)^(-k), the total tail, even multiplied by n, is at most
 n^(C_3+1)*p^(-n)*sum_{k>epsilon*n} C_4^k/k! = o(1).
Dominated convergence now justifies summing the fixed-k limits. The resulting sum equals
 exp(2*alpha*beta)*[-2*alpha*d+2*alpha^2*omega].
Finally c(T+F)/c(T)->exp(2*alpha*beta), proving the displayed relative-ratio limit.

## Exact substitution for the candidate phase
Take r=3, n=33q, m=11q,
 F_A=P22 disjoint union (q-2) copies of K1,10,
 F_B=q copies of P11.
Then
 e_A=10q+1, e_B=10q,
 W_A=20+45(q-2)=45q-70, W_B=9q.
Thus beta=10/33, d=1, omega=12/11, alpha=3/2, and
 n*(c(T+F_B)/c(T+F_A)-1) -> -3+(9/2)*(12/11)=21/11>0.
This proves the strict cycle comparison for ALL sufficiently large q, independently of the finite numerical checks.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_countercycle_comparison.md').write_text(s);print('saved',len(s))

```

from pathlib import Path
s=r'''# Direct edge-extremizer audit for the projection-rendered counterexample

This argument avoids BOTH Simonovits' exact decomposition theorem and the cycle-maximizer structural reduction. Its only graph stability input is ordinary Erdos--Simonovits stability.

Assume the rendered fixed graph H has chi(H)=4, a color-critical vertex, and the exact complete-tripartite bounded-internal-degree realization of the payloads
 K1,11; P12 disjoint union P11; FORK5; each C3,...,C22; one internal edge in each of two distinct classes.
Here FORK5 is the tree with arm lengths 2,1,1. The renderer uses a cell size h>30, so ANY 3-coloring of H with maximum monochromatic degree <=10 is a coordinate projection on the core; the extra root excludes the proper coordinate, leaving precisely the payload tests.

Let G be ANY edge-extremal H-free graph on sufficiently many n vertices. The balanced complete tripartite graph is H-free, so e(G)>=t_3(n). Ordinary stability gives three classes of size n/3+o(n) with o(n^2) internal edges and missing cross-edges. The color-critical-vertex greedy embedding argument allows reassignment of o(n) exceptional vertices so that maximum internal degree is eta*n=o(n), while total missing cross-edges M=o(n^2).

Choose tau->0 with eta=o(tau), M/n^2=o(tau^2), and let B contain vertices with at least tau*n missing cross-neighbors. Then b=|B|<=2M/(tau*n)=o(tau*n). Delete all internal edges incident with B and fill all cross-edges incident with B, obtaining G*. This remains H-free: any new H-copy replaces every new ordinary vertex by a fresh old good vertex in the same class; all required same-class edges incident with new vertices are absent, and every required cross-edge can be secured greedily since good vertices miss only tau*n=o(n) cross-neighbors. The operation gains at least (1-o(1))*tau*n*b missing cross-edges and loses at most eta*n*b+b^2=o(tau*n*b) internal edges. Edge extremality forces B empty. Thus G has maximum missing cross-degree o(n).

Now every SINGLE-CLASS payload can be tested directly by greedy completion: choose its vertices in one class and fill all remaining H-vertices in their prescribed classes, avoiding at most |H|*o(n) nonneighbors. Hence every internal graph avoids K1,11, FORK5, C3,...,C22, and P12 disjoint union P11. Every longer cycle contains the last forest on 23 vertices, so the internal graphs are forests. A tree which is neither a path nor a star contains FORK5: choose a degree>=3 vertex adjacent to a nonleaf. Consequently EVERY internal component is a star on at most 11 vertices or a path on at most 22 vertices.

G is H-saturated by edge maximality. There are finitely many internal component types. Any type occurring linearly many times is cross-complete to EVERY vertex: otherwise add a missing cross-edge incident with one copy; in the created H-copy replace its selected component vertices by a fresh identical component avoiding the o(n) prohibited cross-nonneighbors. This would yield H already in G. Each class therefore has an unlimited reservoir of vertices cross-complete to every other class.

For any internal component C, delete its at most 21 internal edges and fill all its missing cross-edges. This operation is H-free: vertices of the newly independent C can be replaced by fresh reservoir vertices from different components of the same class in any alleged new H-copy. Edge extremality forces C to have at most 21 missing cross-edges. The fresh-copy saturation argument now shows that every type with more than 21|H|+|H| copies is cross-complete to all vertices. All rarer types together form a bounded set R.

The explicit candidate A below has positive linear surplus, so G contains linearly many internal edges. Since R is bounded, some class has a nontrivial repeated normal component; call it active. Its normal edge is cross-complete to all vertices. If either other class had ANY internal edge, the two-class matching payload would embed, using that edge and the active normal edge; all remaining H-vertices are filled from reservoirs. Thus the other two classes are independent.

At this point max internal degree<=10 and there is only one active class. Fill ALL remaining cross-nonedges. The resulting graph is still H-free: by renderer rigidity an H-copy would have to use a coordinate payload, but every single-class payload is absent by the established internal restrictions and the two-class payload is impossible. Hence G already had all cross-edges.

Write m for its active-class size. There can be at most one path component of order >=12; it has order at most 22, and if present every other path has order at most 10. All star components have order at most 11. For large m, the maximum internal edge count is exactly
 f(m)=m-ceil(m/11)+1.
Indeed a component of order<=22 together with all other components of order<=11 forces at least ceil((m-22)/11)+1=ceil(m/11)-1 components, and the bound is attained by one path of order 12,...,22 plus stars of order<=11.

For n=33q put m0=11q. If the three part sizes are m0+d,m0+a,m0+b, with d+a+b=0 and first class active, then relative to balanced classes the edge difference is
 -(d^2+a^2+b^2)/2+d-ceil(d/11).
It is strictly negative unless d=a=b=0: for d>=1 use ceil(d/11)>=1 and a^2+b^2>=d^2/2; for d<=-1 the internal difference d-ceil(d/11)<0 already. Thus the balanced partition is uniquely optimal.

For active size 11q, attaining f(11q)=10q+1 requires exactly q-1 components. One must have order 22 and all remaining q-2 components order 11. The order-22 component is P22; its presence forbids any other P11, so all remaining components are 11-vertex stars. Therefore EVERY edge extremizer is isomorphic to
 A_q=K_{11q,11q,11q} with internal P22 disjoint union (q-2)K1,10 in one class.
In particular ex(n,H)<=t_3(n)+(10/33)n+O(1), and ex(33q,H)=t_3(33q)+10q+1.

The rival B_q puts q copies of P11 in one balanced class. It is H-free by renderer rigidity, and has one fewer edge. The rigorous cycle comparison in agent3_countercycle_comparison.md gives
 33q*(c(B_q)/c(A_q)-1) ->21/11>0.
Thus for all sufficiently large q, NO edge-extremizer is a cycle maximizer. If the renderer is valid, this contradicts the requested exclusion for a=10/33<1/3.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_direct_edge_counteraudit.md').write_text(s);print('saved',len(s))

from pathlib import Path
p=Path('workspace/agent3_countercycle_comparison.md');s=p.read_text()
a=s.index('## Uniform summation justification');b=s.index('## Exact substitution')
s=s[:a]+r'''## Uniform summation justification (strongest, elementary version)
Discard original cycles shorter than n-L_n, with L_n=floor(n/sqrt(log n)). Their total number in ANY n-vertex graph is at most n!*poly(n)/L_n!, which is o(c(T)/n^A) for every fixed A, since c(T)>=n!*p^n*n^(-C). In the concrete phase all internal double-edge contractions have original length at most 23, so they are discarded too.

Let Q_{k,t}^{long} be the kernel restricted to cycles whose original uncontracted length is at least n-L_n. A compressed cycle has length ell>=n-k-L_n, and uses at most m-k vertices of the first class. Thus it has at least
 ell-2*a_used >= n-2m+k-L_n
edges with both ends outside that class. Insert k fixed fresh ordinary vertices of the first class in a fixed order. Each insertion lowers the number of admissible edges by one, so throughout there are at least n-2m-L_n>=n/4 choices (r=3). The output is a cycle of T, and deleting the fixed fresh labels is a unique inverse. Consequently, for EVERY k,t,
 Q_{k,t}^{long}/c(T) <= (4/n)^k.
For general fixed r>=3 replace 4 by any constant larger than r/(r-2).

The nonmatching k-edge count is at most W*binom(e-2,k-2), by marking an adjacent edge pair. Also
 |binom(e,k)-binom(e-d,k)| <= |d|*binom(max(e,e-d),k-1).
Thus the difference of matching counts in the two forests is bounded by this last expression plus (W+W')*binom(e_max-2,k-2), and the sum of all nonmatching counts has the latter bound. Since e,e',W,W'=O(n), after multiplying by n, the orientation factor <=2^k, and the kernel bound (4/n)^k, the difference terms are dominated by
 C_1^k/((k-1)!)+C_2^k/((k-2)!),
a summable series, UNIFORMLY in n and all k. This is a genuine difference-tail estimate: using individual cycle terms would incorrectly lose a factor n.

For fixed k,t the cutoff changes the kernel only by a negligible amount, so its first-order ratio remains (alpha/n)^k. Dominated convergence therefore justifies summing the fixed-k limits computed above. The resulting sum equals
 exp(2*alpha*beta)*[-2*alpha*d+2*alpha^2*omega].
Finally c(T+F)/c(T)->exp(2*alpha*beta), proving the displayed relative-ratio limit.

'''+s[b:]
p.write_text(s);print('updated',len(s))

```

All graphs are finite, simple, and undirected. A graph is $H$-free if it contains no subgraph, not necessarily induced, isomorphic to $H$. Write $e(G)$ for the number of edges of $G$, and $c(G)$ for its number of simple cycles of length at least three, counted without orientation or a chosen starting vertex. For a fixed graph $H$, define
\[
\operatorname{ex}(n,H)=\max\{e(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\},
\qquad
\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)=\max\{c(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\}.
\]
Let $\chi(H)$ denote the minimum number of colors in a proper vertex coloring of $H$. Let $T_{n,r}$ be the complete $r$-partite graph on $n$ vertices whose part sizes differ by at most one, allowing empty parts, and put $t_r(n)=e(T_{n,r})$.

For each integer $r\ge2$, let $\mathcal S_r$ be the set of real numbers $a\ge0$ for which there exist a fixed graph $H$ with $\chi(H)=r+1$, a constant $C\ge0$, and a positive integer $N_0$ satisfying both conditions:

1. For every integer $N\ge N_0$,
\[
\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C.
\]
2. For infinitely many positive integers $n$, there is no $n$-vertex $H$-free graph $G$ satisfying simultaneously
\[
e(G)=\operatorname{ex}(n,H)
\quad\text{and}\quad
c(G)=\operatorname{cyc}(n,H).
\]

The choices of $H,C,N_0$ may depend on $r$ and $a$, but not on $N$ or $n$. Determine $\mathcal S_r$ explicitly for every integer $r\ge2$, including whether its finite boundary points belong to it.

The answer is \mathcal S_2=[0,\infty),\qquad \mathcal S_r=[1/r,\infty)\ \text{for }r\ge3.. This was proved recently, so it is not an open problem: your team's task is to prove it, completely, and to write Python that really computes or checks every step that can be checked. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at any other answer is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving it takes three claims: for every \(a\ge0\), there exist \(H,C,N_0\) with \(\chi(H)=3\) satisfying conditions 1 and 2; for every \(r\ge3\) and \(a\ge1/r\), there exist \(H,C,N_0\) with \(\chi(H)=r+1\) satisfying conditions 1 and 2; and for every \(r\ge3\) and \(0\le a<1/r\), every fixed graph \(H\) with \(\chi(H)=r+1\) for which \(\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C\) for some \(C\ge0\) and all sufficiently large \(N\) has, for every sufficiently large \(n\), an \(n\)-vertex \(H\)-free graph \(G\) satisfying both \(e(G)=\operatorname{ex}(n,H)\) and \(c(G)=\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)\). Split the work, and the pieces of each part, between you on Slack.

Stop only when the team's proof is complete. Before you stop, post on Slack what you proved, and what is left.

### The counterexample in one page

Construct a fixed enormous \(\chi(H)=4\) graph by a “projection renderer.”

- Core: independent blow-up of the categorical product \(K_3^{25}\), every cell has \(h=300\) vertices. Vertex labels `(word in {0,1,2}^25, clone index 0..299)`.
- Core adjacency: two core vertices are adjacent iff their words differ in **every** coordinate.
- 24 payload coordinates, plus a distinguished proper coordinate \(\sigma\).
- Payloads:
  1. \(K_{1,11}\) (12 vertices);
  2. \(P_{12}\sqcup P_{11}\) (23 vertices);
  3. FORK5, the five-vertex tree with arm lengths \(2,1,1\) (5 vertices);
  4. each \(C_k\), \(3\le k\le22\) (250 vertices total);
  5. two disjoint edges, one prescribed monochromatic in color 0 and one in color 1 (4 vertices).
- Total 294 special vertices, allocated as distinct existing core clones; 288 added payload edges.
- For payload \(i\), its vertices have coordinate \(i\) constant 0 (except second edge of two-class payload is constant 1); in EVERY other coordinate their values form a proper 3-coloring \(\kappa_i\) of that payload. Consequently its added edges are monochromatic ONLY in coordinate \(i\).
- Add root \(v\), adjacent to all 300 vertices in three cells: all 24 payload coordinates equal 0, and \(\sigma=0,1,2\), respectively. Thus \(\deg(v)=900\).
- Total
  \[
  |H|=300\cdot3^{25}+1=254186582832901.
  \]
- \(\chi(H)=4\); root \(v\) is color-critical: \(H-v\) is properly 3-colored by \(\sigma\), and all proper 3-colorings of the core are coordinate projections, while every payload projection is blocked by its added edge, and \(\sigma\) is blocked by root’s neighbors of all colors.
- The center \(c\) of the \(K_{1,11}\) payload is ALSO color-critical. Projection onto its coordinate properly colors \(H-c\); reinsert \(c\) gives exactly an internal 11-leaf star (and otherwise isolated vertices in its color class). This bounds internal degree of dense H-free templates by 10.

**Projection rigidity proof:** categorical \(K_r^d\), \(r\ge3\), has only coordinate proper \(r\)-colorings, up to color permutation. Hoffman: adjacency \((J-I)^{\otimes d}\), degree \((r-1)^d\), least eigenvalue \(-(r-1)^{d-1}\), least eigenspace consists of sums of one-coordinate mean-zero functions. Equality for maximum independent sets gives Boolean function \(1/r+\sum_i g_i(x_i)\); a 2×2 rectangle shows only one \(g_i\) can be nonconstant. Hence maximum independent sets are coordinate fibers, and disjoint fibers in a proper r-coloring must use same coordinate.
For a coloring of blow-up whose monochromatic degree is \(\le D=10\), choose a dominant color in each cell. Since \(h>rD\), dominant color occurs \(>D\) times. Adjacent cells cannot have same dominant color. Their dominant coloring is a projection. Any minority-colored vertex has \(>D\) same-colored neighbors in an adjacent cell whose dominant color is that minority color. So EVERY core vertex is forced to the projection. Root excludes \(\sigma\), because it would have 300 same-colored neighbors whichever \(\sigma\)-color it receives. In a payload projection root can choose color 1 or 2, avoiding all N(v) (color 0). Thus any H-embedding into a complete tripartite graph with max internal degree 10 must realize one of the prescribed colored payloads, and conversely any payload permits H-embedding for large host parts.

### Direct classification of ALL edge extremizers (no Simonovits exact theorem needed!)

For this H, all sufficiently large edge extremizers are complete tripartite with one active class containing a star/path forest. This was proved independently by me and agent_2; coordinator audited.

1. Ordinary Erdős–Simonovits H-stability, because edge-extremizer has \(e\ge t_3(n)\), gives 3 balanced classes with \(o(n^2)\) internal/missing-cross edges.
2. Root critical vertex + greedy embedding implies each vertex has \(o(n)\) neighbors in some good class. Reassign o(n) bad vertices to such classes, yielding max internal degree \(\eta n=o(n)\), total missing cross edges \(M=o(n^2)\).
3. Pick \(\tau\to0\), \(\eta=o(\tau)\), \(M/n^2=o(\tau^2)\). Let B = vertices with at least \(\tau n\) missing cross neighbors. Then \(b=|B|=o(\tau n)\).
   Delete all internal edges incident B and cross-complete B (“ordinary replacement”). H-free: any new H-copy can replace its new ordinary vertices by unused old good vertices in same classes, greedily (new vertices have no required same-class edges).
   Adds \((1-o(1))\tau n b\) edges, loses \(\eta nb+b^2=o(\tau nb)\). Edge-extremality forces B empty. So max missing cross-degree \(o(n)\).
4. Single-class payloads can now be tested by greedily filling the remainder of H around any chosen payload:
   - \(K_{1,11}\) forbids internal degree ≥11;
   - FORK5 forbids any internal fork;
   - \(C_3,\dots,C_{22}\), and \(P_{12}\sqcup P_{11}\) forbid EVERY internal cycle (cycles ≥23 contain disjoint P12+P11).
   - Thus ALL internal components are stars of order ≤11 or paths of order ≤22.
5. Edge saturation + many copies of a component type imply those copies are cross-complete to ALL vertices: otherwise add a missing cross edge, take resulting H-copy, replace its selected component by a fresh identical copy avoiding \(h_H o(n)\) cross nonneighbors.
   Each part has a linear-multiplicity type, giving cross-complete reservoirs.
6. Any internal component C can be made independent and cross-completed, staying H-free by replacing its alleged H-images by reservoir vertices. It loses ≤21 edges. Therefore edge-extremality implies TOTAL missing cross edges incident C ≤21.
7. Any component type with \(>22|H|\) copies is now cross-complete to all vertices (at most \(21|H|\) forbidden vertices/copies plus |H| occupied). All rarer types form bounded R. R vertices are automatically ordinary: zero edges to normal components in their own part, complete to normal components of other parts.
8. Candidate below gives positive linear surplus, so an active normal class with many edges exists. The two-class two-edge payload forbids ANY edge in either other class (use normal active edge, cross-complete to rare edge too).
9. Fill every remaining rare–rare cross nonedge. Still H-free by projection rigidity: internal maximum degree 10, one active class, all single-class payloads absent, two-class payload impossible. Thus all cross edges already present.

For active size m large, internal maximum
\[
f(m)=m-\lceil m/11\rceil+1.
\]
At most one path component can have ≥12 vertices, and it has ≤22; all other components ≤11, so at least \(\lceil(m-22)/11\rceil+1=\lceil m/11\rceil-1\) components. Achieve by one path order 12..22 + stars ≤11.

For n=33q, balanced active size m=11q is uniquely optimal. Part sizes `(m+d,m+a,m+b)`, d+a+b=0, edge difference relative balanced phase:
\[
-\frac{d^2+a^2+b^2}{2}+d-\lceil d/11\rceil<0
\]
unless d=a=b=0.
At active size 11q, equality f=10q+1 requires q−1 components: one \(P_{22}\), and q−2 components of size11. P22 forbids any other P11, hence all remaining components are 11-vertex stars \(K_{1,10}\).

Thus **EVERY** edge extremizer for n=33q is
\[
A_q=T_{33q,3}+\left(P_{22}\sqcup(q-2)K_{1,10}\right)
\]
inside one part, and \(e(A_q)=t_3(33q)+10q+1\).
For all sufficiently large n,
\[
\mathrm{ex}(n,H)\le t_3(n)+\frac{10}{33}n+2.
\]
Proof C=2: f(m)≤10m/11+1, put δ=m−n/3, cross loss≥3δ²/4, \(n^2/3-t_3(n)\le1/3\), maximum of `(10/11)δ−3δ²/4` is100/363, so constant `<2`.

Rival
\[
B_q=T_{33q,3}+qP_{11}
\]
inside one part is H-free by projection rigidity, has one fewer edge, but MORE cycles for every sufficiently large q:
\[
33q\left(\frac{c(B_q)}{c(A_q)}-1\right)\longrightarrow \frac{21}{11}>0.
\]
Hence this H witnesses \(10/33\in\mathcal S_3\), contradicting prescribed exclusion \(a<1/3\).

## My rigorous ALL-cycle comparison proof (latest, strongest version)

File `workspace/agent3_countercycle_comparison.md`, latest 4464 chars. It generalizes:
fixed r≥3, n=rm, bounded-degree forests F,F′ inside first part, eF/n,eF′/n→β, eF−eF′=d fixed, `(W(F)−W(F′))/n→ω`, \(W=\sum\binom{d_F(v)}2\), α=r/(r−1). Then
\[
n\left(\frac{c(T+F')}{c(T+F)}-1\right)\to -2\alpha d+2\alpha^2\omega.
\]

Exact decomposition of cycles via internal path forests:
- \(N_{k,t}(F)\) counts k-edge path forests with t nontrivial components.
- Kernel \(R_{k,t}\): cycles of \(K_{m-k,m,\ldots,m}\) containing t specified first-class supervertices.
- c(T+F) = cT + ∑ 2^t N_{k,t} R_{k,t} + E_F.
- E_F = cycles contracting to 2-vertex double edge (one internal path + one outside vertex), at most exp(O(n)), negligible; in our actual phases original length≤23.
- For fixed k,t, \(R_{k,t}/cT\sim(\alpha/n)^k\). This follows from fixed complete multipartite contraction ratio via simple Fourier/Markov color-word local Gaussian computation; required specified vertices included with probability 1−O(1/n) by ordinary insertion.
- Fixed k overlap counts:
  \[
  N_{k,k}=\binom e k-W\binom{e-2}{k-2}+O_k(n^{k-2}),
  \]
  \[
  N_{k,k-1}=W\binom{e-2}{k-2}+O_k(n^{k-2}),
  \]
  all t≤k−2 contribute O_k(n^{k−2}).
- Thus fixed k contribution to n(cF′−cF)/cT tends
  \[
  2^k\alpha^k\left[-d\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}
       +\frac{\omega}{2}\frac{\beta^{k-2}}{(k-2)!}\right].
  \]

**Uniform difference tails, audited agent_1 #304 / me #307:**
- Discard ORIGINAL cycles shorter than n−L, L=floor(n/√logn). ANY graph has at most `n!*poly(n)/L!` such cycles = o(cT/n^A) for every A.
- For compressed long cycle with k contracted edges, length ℓ≥n−k−L and a≤m−k first-class used vertices. Edges with both ends outside first class ≥ℓ−2a≥n−2m+k−L.
- Reinsert k fixed fresh ordinary first-class vertices in fixed order. Every insertion decreases count of such edges by one; always ≥n−2m−L≥n/4 for r=3. Output cycles in T; unique inverse delete fixed labels. So **for all k,t**
  \[
  Q^{long}_{k,t}/cT\le(4/n)^k.
  \]
  General r replace4 by constant>r/(r−2).
- Nonmatching k-edge sets ≤W binom(e−2,k−2) by marking adjacent pair.
- Matching count DIFFERENCE bounded by
  \[
  |d|\binom{e_{\max}}{k-1}+(W+W')\binom{e_{\max}-2}{k-2}.
  \]
- Multiply by n, orientations≤2^k and uniform kernel `(4/n)^k`: dominated by summable
  `C1^k/(k−1)! + C2^k/(k−2)!`.
- DCT sum fixed k limits = `exp(2αβ)*(-2αd+2α²ω)`.
- Leading c(T+F)/cT→exp(2αβ) also follows DIRECTLY from same decomposition/kernel: fixed k matchings lead, individual terms ≤C^k/k!, short cycles negligible. **No general Dirac Hamilton count, no second-order saddle point needed.**
- For our A/B:
  `eA=10q+1`, `eB=10q`;
  `WA=20+45(q−2)=45q−70`, `WB=9q`;
  β=10/33, d=1, ω=12/11, α=3/2,
  limit = `−3+(9/2)*(12/11)=21/11`.

Base complete multipartite ratio proof in `workspace/agent3_cycle_asymptotics.md`:
- Matrix M(z)ij=zj if i≠j else0.
- cyclic color strings generating `tr M(z)^n`, open `1^T diag(z) M(z)^(n−1)1`.
- r≥3 simple leading eigen r−1, others−1.
- stationary chain uniform, transition rho=−1/(r−1) on mean-zero functions, occupation covariance `(r−2)/r²` identity on sum-zero subspace, positive.
- Fourier coefficient Gaussian near θ0; outside θ0 spectral radius strictly<r−1 (equality forces all phases equal, fix θr0).
- For counts n_i=n/r+O(1), cyclic count `K_r (r−1)^n n^{-(r−1)/2}(1+o1)` independent bounded displacement.
- open/cyclic ratio→r/(r−1).
- h multipartite = A_cyc∏n_i!/(2n).
- fixed k contractions first class ratio ∼[r/((r−1)n)]^k.
- Complete multipartite ALL-cycle/Hamilton ratio→exp(r/(r−1)) by fixed omitted vertices j ratio p^-j/j! plus insertion factorial domination, p=(r−1)/r.
- For this all-cycle base ratio, no heavy Dirac result: ONE proper Hamilton color word gives h≥∏n_i!/(2n)≥n!r^-n/(2n); short factorial tail negligible. For fixed bounded displacements, min degree pn−O1 ensures omitted vertices can be inserted Ω(n) ways.

from pathlib import Path
p=Path('workspace/agent3_countercycle_comparison.md');s=p.read_text()
a=s.index('## Uniform summation justification');b=s.index('## Exact substitution')
s=s[:a]+r'''## Uniform summation justification (strongest, elementary version)
Discard original cycles shorter than n-L_n, with L_n=floor(n/sqrt(log n)). Their total number in ANY n-vertex graph is at most n!*poly(n)/L_n!, which is o(c(T)/n^A) for every fixed A, since c(T)>=n!*p^n*n^(-C). In the concrete phase all internal double-edge contractions have original length at most 23, so they are discarded too.

Let Q_{k,t}^{long} be the kernel restricted to cycles whose original uncontracted length is at least n-L_n. A compressed cycle has length ell>=n-k-L_n, and uses at most m-k vertices of the first class. Thus it has at least
 ell-2*a_used >= n-2m+k-L_n
edges with both ends outside that class. Insert k fixed fresh ordinary vertices of the first class in a fixed order. Each insertion lowers the number of admissible edges by one, so throughout there are at least n-2m-L_n>=n/4 choices (r=3). The output is a cycle of T, and deleting the fixed fresh labels is a unique inverse. Consequently, for EVERY k,t,
 Q_{k,t}^{long}/c(T) <= (4/n)^k.
For general fixed r>=3 replace 4 by any constant larger than r/(r-2).

The nonmatching k-edge count is at most W*binom(e-2,k-2), by marking an adjacent edge pair. Also
 |binom(e,k)-binom(e-d,k)| <= |d|*binom(max(e,e-d),k-1).
Thus the difference of matching counts in the two forests is bounded by this last expression plus (W+W')*binom(e_max-2,k-2), and the sum of all nonmatching counts has the latter bound. Since e,e',W,W'=O(n), after multiplying by n, the orientation factor <=2^k, and the kernel bound (4/n)^k, the difference terms are dominated by
 C_1^k/((k-1)!)+C_2^k/((k-2)!),
a summable series, UNIFORMLY in n and all k. This is a genuine difference-tail estimate: using individual cycle terms would incorrectly lose a factor n.

For fixed k,t the cutoff changes the kernel only by a negligible amount, so its first-order ratio remains (alpha/n)^k. Dominated convergence therefore justifies summing the fixed-k limits computed above. The resulting sum equals
 exp(2*alpha*beta)*[-2*alpha*d+2*alpha^2*omega].
Finally c(T+F)/c(T)->exp(2*alpha*beta), proving the displayed relative-ratio limit.

'''+s[b:]
p.write_text(s);print('updated',len(s))

```
